ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u14	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! Marshal of the Sejm at the 71st meeting, on the basis of Article. 87. 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, he sent the above draft to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications for consideration. The Extraordinary Commission considered the project on 20 November 2018. Let me remind you that this project concerns a very important issue, so-called. Anatolysm, i.e. interest on interest. In this respect, the committee recommends that the draft no. 2876 be adopted without amendments. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u15	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Please, Mr Marek Ast, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u16	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I am pleased to present my position on the report of the committee on the presidential draft law amending the Civil Code Act, print No 2876 and 3006. The draft provides for the deletion from the Civil Code of the provision contained in Article. 482 § 2, i.e. simply abolishing this unjustified advantage for credit institutions, banks regarding the calculation of interest on late interest. After this change, there would be only two exceptions to the principle of anatolysm: one when the parties have agreed to capitalise on interest, and the other when this capitalization takes place at the time of the lawsuit. During the committee's work, there was doubts about the inter-temporal rule contained in the draft. The suggestion was to clarify more precisely the rules that would apply to the new rules. It is proposed - and I will make such an amendment on behalf of the club - to Article. 2 is replaced by the following: The existing provisions shall apply to interest due before the date of entry into force of this Law. Here we have a clarification of the wording of the inter-temporal regulation and at the same time an indication that the existing provisions will apply only to interest due until the date of entry into force of the amending law. In this situation, on behalf of the club, I request that the bill be adopted with this amendment tabled. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u17	Thank you very much. Please, Mr Zdzisław Gawlik, Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u18	Mr. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform wanted to present a position on amending the Civil Code Act in print No 2876. As the previous speakers mentioned, the proposed amendment concerns a certain modification of the rules on the principle of anatomy in the system of Polish law. These rules are currently provided for in Article. 482 Civil Code, which bans so-called. Anatolysm, i.e. making an appointment in advance for payment of interest on interest outstanding. It's the so-called. compound percentage. This principle prevents the debtor's debt from multiplying indefinitely. This provision is absolutely binding; beyond its scope, it is only the exceptions laid down in the Civil Code. These exceptions for today are three: the situation where the admissibility of contractual interest collection regulation is provided for in the agreement between the parties, secondly, the situation where the creditor has brought an action after interest and, thirdly, the case of long-term loans granted by credit institutions. It was a group of privileged entities which, by law, had the right to charge interest on late interest. The amendment provides for the repeal of one of these exceptions, namely the favour of those credit institutions which granted long-term loans. The repeal of one of these exceptions essentially means a return to the rule of law that was in force under the Code of Obligations in the field of the principle of anatomy, and that is to say, Article. § 249 § 1 Code of Commitments, which gave the possibility to calculate interest for late interest only from the moment of filing for it, unless by the will of the parties the interest was capitalised upon the backlog. Attention should be paid to the return to the rules of the interwar period in this respect, on § 2 of the provision referred to in Article. 249, which indicated that this provision does not prejudice different commercial rules and practices. I think that this is also a good regulation, which is worth leaning on when we are thinking comprehensively about how to regulate the principle of anatoism in the system of Polish law. On behalf of the club, I can support this amendment to the Civil Code, but seeing the need for further work on the shape of the principle of anatolyzm in the system of Polish law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u31	Thank you very much, Minister. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u32	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats a position on the report of the committee on the draft law presented by the President of the Republic of Poland amending the Civil Code Act, prints no. 2876 and 3006. The project envisages eliminating interest rates, i.e. the so-called interest rates. Anatolysm. The proposal aims to remove from our legal system the exception to the ban on anatomy contained in the article. 482 § 2 of the Civil Code, the purpose of which is to eliminate the unjustified preference of credit institutions in the calculation of interest on late interest on the basis of this provision. Currently, the Civil Code provides for three exceptions to the prohibition of anatolycism. The first concerns the case where the parties have already agreed after the rise of interest on their capitalization. The second exception concerns the capital injections made after an outstanding interest claim has been brought. The third exception is subjective and refers to credit institutions. According to Art. 482 § 2 of the Civil Code is allowed to collect interest on late interest due on long-term loans granted by credit institutions. In the case of the first two exceptions, the requirement for the calculation of interest is that there is a backlog and, in the case of the third exception, such provision is permitted already in the long-term loan agreement. The debtor of a credit institution under a long-term loan agreement is therefore not protected on general terms against overly restrictive interest for late payment and the credit institution itself is privileged in legal transactions in this respect. One of the situations where anatolycism is currently applicable is the granting of a long-term loan by the credit institution. However, both of these terms are inaccurately explained, which in practice leads to a number of interpretational abuses and, consequently, too frequent application of loan interest capitalisations. This project aims to improve the position of consumers in their relations with banks and other credit institutions. Its introduction will counter the increase in the debt of clients of credit institutions and fall into the so-called. the debt loop for the possibility of making an appointment in advance for payment of interest on late interest, so-called. interest paid, without bringing an action. High Izbo! The proposed changes should be assessed positively, as they should reduce the practice of loan companies which often increase the burden on debtors in a way that insults all standards. The implementation of the Act will counter the increase in the debt of clients of credit institutions and fall into the so-called. the debt loop for the possibility of making an appointment in advance for payment of interest on late interest, so-called. the compound percentage. However, it should be borne in mind that the repeal of the Article. 482 § 2 will not result in a credit institution not being able to claim interest on outstanding interest, but will be subject to prior action.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u33	In view of the fact that, at the time of the second reading, an amendment was notified to the proposed amendment, pursuant to Article. OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm I am directing this draft law to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications in order to submit a report. We proceed with the examination of item 2 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Law on Special Solutions for the Implementation of the Budget Law for 2018 (Print No 3054). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr. Tomasz Robaczyński to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u34	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present to you a draft law amending the Law on Special Solutions for the Implementation of the Budget Law for 2018. The regulations contained in this project are related to the implementation of this year's state budget and will allow for the allocation of part of the blocked funds by individual directors in accordance with the Public Finance Act for the purposes indicated in the draft law. These savings in planned expenditure are mainly due to the good labour market situation and the lower demand for subsidies from the State budget to the Social Insurance Fund, as well as to the lower need for own resources from the European Union, which is mainly linked to the settlement in 2018 of overpayments for previous years by reducing monthly contributions. In fact, the Act restructures the expenditure of the State budget under the measures available under the blockades I mentioned, while at the same time does not change the macroeconomic parameters, i.e. does not increase the expenditure of the public finance sector and thus does not worsen the outcome of the public finance sector in 2018. Unlike in previous years, this change is precisely due to good economic situation and not to the need to increase the deficit limit of the state budget due to the economic situation. The savings we are talking about, according to the solutions we are designing, are to be spent, among others. for additional funds for the National Health Fund to increase the reserve fund of PLN 1.8 billion, which can be used by the National Health Fund after obtaining the approval of the Minister of Finance and the Minister of Health. In addition, with regard to the National Health Fund, we also provide for the possibility to provide a special grant to the NFZ of over PLN 320 million in 2018 to finance the tasks referred to in Article. Paragraph 97. 3 points 2a, 3 and 3b of the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare. This provision aims to pay in lump sum for the provision of health care for persons who do not have health insurance and who should be provided under the provisions of that Law. This applies in particular to uninsured children. The project also includes provisions concerning the financing of mining restructuring of PLN 150 million and the increase of funds for the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture for the implementation of aid to agricultural holdings and agricultural producers with damage caused by drought or flooding in the current year. It is planned to allocate PLN 715.5 million for this purpose. Another solution envisaged in the project is to allow the funds of PLN 1.8 billion to be allocated by the State Treasury to take over the shares of PKP Polskie Linie Kolejowe SA, which will improve the ownership structure, where the State Treasury consistently strives to cover 100% the national action of the railway infrastructure manager. The increase in share capital will also contribute to strengthening the company implementing investment projects under the government scheme. A provision is also proposed which provides for the possibility of allocating up to PLN 2100 million from the State budget to the Reprivatisation Fund referred to in the Commercialisation Act and certain rights of employees. This appropriation is intended to cover the implementation of statutory tasks and thus allow investment projects to be carried out, as an additional mechanism for an active economic impact. The project itself also provides for an amendment to the Act on the Self-Government Road Fund, which is to enable the granting of a special subsidy from the budget of the State from the budgetary section of which the Minister responsible for Transport is responsible for carrying out the tasks of the Fund in 2018. This action aims to start the operational functioning of the fund as soon as possible, which was created recently and thus reduce the expectation of the start of the investment process on local roads. The transfer of PLN 1100 million in 2018 will speed up this process. In addition, the proposal also proposes to increase the limit on State budget expenditure for the financing of statutory allowances for public transport by rail for parents and spouses of parents holding an important Large Family Charter by an amount of 274 000. of the amount of 18 260 thousand. up to the amount of 21 million. In conclusion, it is planned that the total transfers of appropriations under this year's budget for the implementation of the tasks mentioned in the draft law will amount to about PLN 8 billion. The regulations presented in the draft law do not jeopardise the performance of the tasks of the directors, but will allow for a more efficient use of funds in this year's state budget. As I have said, the transfer of these funds does not change the deficit of the public finance sector, and I therefore ask the High House to adopt the bill submitted. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u71	Thank you. Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Finance Tomasz Robaczyński has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u72	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! To answer the last of the questions, first of all, I wanted to answer the question of how these measures came about, because here there were voices that it had not been explained. I mean, the money comes from a blockage in the state budget. The mechanism is that funds which, according to the Public Finance Act, will not be used in the State budget, may be blocked in accordance with that law, i.e. it is known that these expenditures will no longer be made in a given year. First of all, these blockades, as I said in my first speech, relate to the funds allocated to the Social Insurance Fund. That is because of the very good situation on the labour market, the FUS does not need to be co-financed from the State budget in this respect and, therefore, the measures that were planned for the payment of benefits by the FUS will not be implemented. Therefore, the ZUS has blocked these measures. In addition, as I also said, measures have been blocked in connection with the non-fulfilment of... [[Voice from the room: Investment.]] Not an investment, sir, but not a full contribution to the European Union, because this contribution was overpaid last year. The mechanism is that the premium shall be reduced in the following year if that contribution exceeds the amounts foreseen for the year in question. Because for the previous year we had overpaid this contribution, this year the measures that were foreseen may also be blocked. The funds of PLN 1 billion for the service of national debt were also blocked due to the steadily decreasing level of the Treasury debt. Of course, there are more roadblocks. These are the main ones. Some tasks, of course, as Mrs Skowrońska said, have not been carried out, but these are not tasks of the same scale as the one I was talking about in the case of the Social Security Office or contributions to the European Union. These are normal savings that always arise. With annual budgets of PLN 20, 30, 40 billion, savings of this kind are always generated at certain points, and these unrealised expenditures are being blocked by individual directors and put at the disposal of the Minister of Finance. So that they come from this, this is the source of this amendment. Therefore, it is difficult to answer the question of who will suffer the consequences for unfulfilled tasks. I mean, as far as these are tasks that are naturally not realized or that are natural savings, it is obviously difficult to talk about responsibility here. If this is really something that requires this kind of action, then the Prime Minister can, of course, always have consequences, but we do not see in the case of such tasks, to be honest, within the framework of these blockades which are foreseen here, within the framework of this project, that there may be some special omissions from the directors and the non-fulfilment of investments in particular. That is also what Mr Silchoń asked. As regards the deficit of the state budget and what Mr Silchoń also asked, i.e. that most countries or a large part of countries - maybe not most, but a large part - will have a surplus in the state budget, as I said, whether there is a surplus in the state budget itself or not, this deficit parameter of the state budget itself is not the most important in the context of the sustainability of all public finances. The most important is the public finance sector deficit, and in this respect this amendment does not foresee a deterioration of the outcome of the public finance sector, because in fact all these transfers are shifts within the public finance sector, i.e. between the state budget and those in the public finance sector. Therefore, these flows do not result in an increase in the deficit of the public finance sector, which is precisely the parameter to which the European Commission particularly draws attention, which may be implementing appropriate procedures if such parameters are exceeded. As for the 2.1 billion for the Reprivatisation Fund, what these funds are allocated to, it is difficult to agree that buying out the remaining shares in the State Treasury companies makes no sense when the State Treasury already owns 50% of the shares in the company. action or enough action to have a decisive vote. Of course, the entire ownership structure of the company affects, for example, the revenue of the state budget, i.e. dividends or profit payments received from these companies, provided that the State Treasury owns them. Of course, these measures or good management of these companies, the good results of these companies also affect the structure of the state budget and the revenue to the state budget, so that it is difficult to agree that buying such shares does not make sense. In addition, a list of investments in key sectors of the economy is being prepared in the Chancellery of the President of the Council of Ministers, which is located in the Responsible Development Strategy. These are the sectors that have the greatest impact on economic development. I do not know how to give specific titles or specific company names, because these works are still ongoing, but it will be the funds intended to increase the capital of these entities, which are crucial for the Responsible Development Strategy and thus for the development of the Polish economy. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u78	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to you a draft law on the promotion of electricity from high-efficiency cogeneration, contained in print No. 3052. The aim of the proposed law is to implement a new support mechanism for high-efficiency cogeneration electricity, which is to replace a system based on certificates of origin which expires at the end of this year. The proposed mechanism will ensure both the stimulation of the construction of new cogeneration units, as support for units with a total capacity of 5.1 is envisaged in the perspective of 2028. GW electric and maintenance of electricity production from high-efficiency cogeneration in existing units which could not function without support due to a financial gap in operating costs. The proposed support system will be dedicated to a large extent for the construction of cogeneration units with installed electricity capacity of up to 50 MWe. However, the mechanism also provides for the possibility of supporting production in larger units, i.e. over 50 MW, e.g. due to the need to replace older sources working for large district heating systems scheduled to be discontinued as a result of the end of the so-called period. district heating derogation from the Industrial Emissions Directive, newer ones, with lower emissions of CO2, SOx, NOx, dust and other substances. One of the main objectives of implementing the new mechanism is the need to improve the air quality in cities, which can be ensured through the development of system heating based on the least emission source. Therefore, the support for all electricity produced in high-efficiency cogeneration, which has been put on the grid and sold, will concern cogeneration units which provide a minimum of 70% utility heat from cogeneration to the public heating network. For entry units less than 70% the heat generated will be supported by electricity in proportion to the share of heat introduced into the grid in the heat produced. In addition, support will only be granted to those units for which the unit carbon dioxide emission factor, so-called. The EPS will be a maximum of 450 kg CO2 per MWh produced energy, i.e. combined electricity and heat. The support instrument will be - and here are several instruments - first, the guaranteed bonus. She's gonna have a little one, I mean. less than 1 MW, cogeneration units, both new and significantly upgraded and upgraded existing. It will also benefit existing and upgraded cogeneration units from a power range of 1 to 50. It shall be granted after the operator of the unit has made an appropriate request and has examined it by the President of the URE. The individual guarantee premium in the case of existing and upgraded cogeneration units will be above 50 MW, which will be calculated individually by the President of the URE and granted after the operator has submitted an appropriate application. The cogeneration premium for new and significantly upgraded cogeneration units from capacity range 1 to 50 MWe will be available under the auction procedure, which must be preceded by a pre-qualification of the unit concerned. The individual cogeneration premium applies to new units and significantly upgraded cogeneration units, above 50 MWe. It is possible to obtain it under the recruitment procedure, in which it will be calculated individually by the President of the URE. It must also be preceded by a pre-qualification of the individual in this procedure. In addition, aid for cogeneration units with installed electricity capacity above 300 MW will be subject to individual notification to the European Commission. Support will be provided for new and significantly modernised cogeneration units for a period of 15 years, and for upgraded cogeneration units for a period of between 5 and 7 years, this period will depend on the level of expenditure on modernisation investments. In the case of existing cogeneration units, a maximum of 15 years from the date on which the first certificate of origin from cogeneration was obtained in the past, but this calculation will be excluded from the calculation, there will be a period of interruption in the functioning of the system of certificates of origin in 2013 and at the beginning of 2014. The bill provides for appropriate solutions to prevent oversupport, among others. prevents the combination of different operational support schemes for the same electricity unit and takes into account the investment aid received, i.e. in the case of premiums which also cover the capital expenditure incurred. The support system will be financed by the cogeneration fee of electricity system operators, which is different from today's. This also means that the operating costs of the system support will be shown transparently on the electricity bill. The revenue from these charges will be transferred by operators to the clearing operator responsible for clearing fees and payment of support. The fees will be fixed annually by the Minister for Energy by means of a regulation based on the level of the operating costs of the system. In 2019 the rate of the cogeneration levy, taking into account the discount for energy-intensive enterprises, will be PLN 1,58/1 MWh. Here it is worth mentioning that this amount is many times lower than the unit cost of today's functioning support system. The support mechanism implemented in the draft law was subject to agreements with the European Commission. The prenotification process has been carried out, so it is not something that will surprise the European Commission, but the work is already completed. In conclusion, I will say that the implementation of a new electricity support mechanism from high-efficiency cogeneration enabling the further development of cogeneration in Poland will significantly affect the security of electricity supply to the national electricity system and heat to customers connected to heating systems, as well as the improvement of air quality. It is very important that the contested law can enter into force from 1 January 2019 and make it possible to exploit the potential of high-efficiency cogeneration by maintaining production in units for which support is necessary to function, due to the financial gap mentioned above and to avoid what took place in 2013 and early 2014. Thank you very much.

Hence my question: Is it a good legal practice, is it a show of respect for Members who are not representatives of the government, that in 24 and 48 hours the bill is so important from the point of view of Polish energy, air quality in Poland, but also the future to process in such a short time? Was it possible to think earlier - because I understand that you control which laws expire, which need to be extended - and to proceed with such a bill, as well as the appointment of a committee, a subcommittee in the Sejm, but this time to the point? The project proposes replacing the current electricity support mechanism with a high-efficiency cogeneration system to support the construction of new generation capacity in cogeneration. This mechanism is intended to guarantee investors who won the auction a fixed premium on the price of electricity. The promotion of electricity from high-efficiency cogeneration is to take place in four categories, you write this in the law and in the explanatory memorandum. The costs of support schemes are to be spread across all end-users of the whole system. Hence my question and my request to the Minister to present the costs for the final recipients of the electricity system. During the public consultation of the draft law, there were numerous doubts, which I hope the Minister will dispel. I'll give you a few of them, because I'll honestly admit that it's not possible to get acquainted with the entire bill in such a short time. There was a concern that the bill skipped the possibility of support for renewable energy sources, hence my question as to whether the draft law excludes or not the possibility of additional award of installations using other operational forms of aid, i.e. certificate of origin, fixed purchase price or coverage of the negative balance. The Chamber of Industrial Energy and Energy Recipients also pointed out that this bill significantly reduces the possibility of maintaining the viability of industrial heat and power plants with process gases, as well as the methane released and captured by bottom mining robots. That's why my question is, isn't it? It has also been noted that the bill introduces the risk of a lack of stimulus for the development of cogeneration in small, inefficient heating systems. Minister! Why so short proceding time? I would like to ask you to resolve your doubts and, above all, to answer the costs for the recipients, that is, all of us, when it comes to the introduction of this system. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u107	The list of Members registered in the vote has been exhausted. Please take the floor of the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Energy, Mr. Tomasz Dąbrowski.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u108	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! There's been a lot of questions here. Some of the votes were positive voices, voices of support for the purposes of the bill, which is extremely pleasing. I will try to address all the questions and comments that have been raised during the speeches and in the question section. Firstly, the question of the fact that the bill appeared in the Seym quite unexpectedly. Here I will draw attention to the fact that the work on the project has been practically ongoing since last year, since the autumn of last year. Publicly known versions of the project were already available at the beginning of April this year. This discussion is going on all the time. The project was modified. The final form has been established following discussions in the Council of Ministers. As far as the cost of possible transformation related to the system is concerned, we have shown all costs per year in the OSR, per group of customers. Therefore, the same examination of the list in Annex 4 to the OSR will allow for an answer to the question of how much this costs in individual years, as expected, what capacity is planned to join, how much this will cost an individual customer. There was also a thread of a fading electricity market, a falling volume on the energy exchange in the context of cogeneration. Here I would like to remind you that from the very beginning, when the stock exchange obligation was introduced into the legislation, cogeneration was excluded from the stock exchange obligation and here, nothing changes in this respect. The auction system itself was further criticised in the questions and speeches. NIK was indicated for the RES auction system. Here I would like to point out that this support system is partly based on an auction system for which lessons have been learned from the functioning of existing auction systems: and the RES auction system, and in terms of the power market - notabene is currently being Auctioned - and among others. In order to eliminate certain imperfections, greater involvement of the regulator has been introduced. Of course, there was also a charge in the questions that bureaucracy and costs were increasing. The question here, however, is whether we want to have a targeted system that precisely supports those who need support or a proverbial free American, as in the case of certificates of origin, a system that supports a little blindly, and also leads to oversupport, i.e. support where it is not needed at all. And here, I'll pay attention to one thing too. They asked how much it would cost the audience, how much we would pay for this transformation. I'll tell you right away, the question is wrong, 'cause you'd have to ask how much less we're gonna pay. Well, this is all contained in the OSR - today's system of support in the form of certificates of origin costs us, i.e. all recipients, 1.5 billion PLN per year. The proposed support system will cost PLN 207 million next year, i.e. we will pay 1.3 billion less. This should therefore be enjoyed by individual customers and energy-intensive consumers. As far as the next question is concerned, as soon as possible, regarding the energy-intensive allowances provided for in this project, the cogeneration subsidies, I will immediately say that this part will be notified separately and we have not yet obtained the Commission's formal or informal consent. This process is ongoing, which is why in a few weeks we'll be able to tell you when it's gonna happen. However, the calculations indicate that the introduction of these reductions will result in a difference in the rate for the final customer of 18 gr compared to 1 MWh. Questions have been raised regarding support for RES under this law. Here I would like to say that this is a law on the promotion of energy from cogeneration, not renewable energy sources, and all the elements related to the promotion of energy from renewable sources are contained in another law, which we are also updating on the occasion of this law, so as to take into account the specificities of the previously mentioned biogas plants in this system. As regards the issue of the entire efficiency balance that appears here, I would like to say that the whole support scheme for the guaranteed premium, the individual premium, the cogeneration premium, the individual cogeneration premium is financed precisely by the cogeneration fee collected within the whole system, at exactly the amount resulting from the costs of the scheme, while the increased costs of the bodies involved in the scheme, i.e. the President of the URE and the entire operating system and the Minister for Energy, which will therefore have more tasks in determining the many parameters needed to ensure that this support system functions precisely, will actually be covered by the budget, but in comparison with the total savings generated by optimising the system, this is a microscopic cost, and this allows for a more targeted targeting or dedicating of this support. There was also a voice about whether this law supports the construction of the infrastructure needed to transport heat. This law does not serve to support the construction of infrastructure. Other instruments are designed for this purpose, which are already in operation or are being updated or modified. One of the most important instruments available at the moment is the Infrastructure and Environment Operational Programme, which is the whole action to support district heating networks, which is the whole infrastructure part. The Act on Thermomodernisation also provides for the possibility of modernization on district heating hubs in order to achieve greater energy efficiency. There are various types of energy consulting, which has among others. help to prepare appropriate projects or make decisions so that they are optimal. The questions also concerned the auctions and the volume of power that we are planning. All the figures given here are based on actual volumes, collected during work and inter-ministerial consultations and broad public consultations. We have consulted this project with chambers, we have received a number of requests both for the text itself and for the data that have been used here. On this basis, the magnitudes that are included in the impact assessment have been determined. We predict that the first auction will take place around the middle of next year. We also predict that these sizes, namely 5 GW, will be around 2028. There was also a question about the smallest and medium-sized cogeneration units in local government units, what decisions should be made: whether modernization or completely new investments. Here, in fact, each operator of the unit should calculate itself, with which solution will get the best results in a given location. Cogeneration has to do with this, that there is not one standard or model solution that would serve just as well, equally to everyone, because here a lot depends on what is the heat demand profile, what are the possibilities of electricity or heat entering the grid. Taking all these elements into account, each operator should decide whether to upgrade, as this law allows, or to construct a completely new entity. The question was also whether the guaranteed bonus actually guarantees that you will get the size that results... the question about the size of the guaranteed premium. Here the procedure is that the application is made and it is assessed by the regulator in order to meet the requirements set as a condition for obtaining this bonus. If these requirements are met, the response is positive and the premium is granted in the amount in which it is determined. There were still questions about connecting to the network or modifying what is today - whether it would be an obligation to join. I would remind you that, at the moment, there are certain provisions governing the obligation to connect to the network, and we are still planning to extend this obligation, which is today laid down in the provisions of the Article, in the context of these provisions. 7b Energy Law. I think I've exhausted all my questions. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u123	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform will present our position on the Commission's draft law amending the Police Act, print No. 3032. In fact, this project assumes amendment only of Article. Article 96 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The 4th Police Act. The provision, as already mentioned here, concerns the cost of accommodation of a police officer transferred from office to service in another village who did not fire an apartment or a house in the previous place of duty. Currently, these costs are borne by the police, but no longer than for a period of 2 years from the date of allocation of the temporary accommodation. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs proposes that this burden rests on the police all the time the officer is serving in another village. The reason for the transfer from office to work in another locality is the broad interest of the service, and indeed large staff shortages. Such a transfer in addition to new professional challenges for a police officer also means the need to reorganize his personal life, which is an extremely high price. If this separation lasts longer than 2 years, then there really is no reason for a policeman to pay himself for renting a temporary apartment away from his family. In the Polish police almost 6 thousand are missing. Officers. According to the police chief's records as of 1 July this year, there were 6276 vacancies throughout the country. It should be noted that more officers have resigned from the service than have joined it. By the end of July, 2822 people left and 1804 were accepted. There's never been a shortage of personnel in the police. Always the number of officers deciding to leave was close to the number of newcomers. It will not be easy to fill this gap, and the process may take several years. About 5.5 thousand were planned to be admitted to the police this year. persons. In 10 months of this year, more than 15,000 registered. Those willing to serve, but only 4,000 were successfully recruited. persons. 75% The starters are out. It also turns out that police officers often resign from the service at the basic stage of vocational training, and in the first half of this year there were 135 such cases. The human resources situation is therefore difficult. The police themselves admit that the shortcomings are due to a drop in unemployment. The private sector pays much better, gives better conditions and police work has ceased to be attractive, especially for high-end specialists. Unfortunately, the government, including the department of home affairs, did not respond in time. After several months of protests that took a harsh form at the beginning of November, the government was cornered and the crisis had been resolved for the time being. However, in a situation still difficult, the maximum use of staff reserves is very important. In practice, this means distributing police officers with high qualifications: knowledge, skills or experience in the ministry to places where there are shortcomings. It is understandable that they should be compensated for any inconvenience and additional financial burden resulting from the decision to transfer to another locality. Expenditure resulting from the entry into force of this law is estimated at nearly 1850 thousand. annually and will be covered by the police budget. As a club, we have no doubt that this is a fully justified proposal to amend the law, and the Civic Platform will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Thirdly, if there is a gap in the provisions of the Act on metropolitan union formation, even though it concerns today, one space, while respecting the principles of the correctness of lawmaking, the gap which has arisen must be corrected and the omission fixed. Fourthly, also taking into account the rotation of the staff, i.e. the process of replacing one person with another, we must also remember that it refers more functionally to the composition of the board than to the personnel composition. Therefore, in a permanent manner, it must refer to functional characteristics, not personal characteristics - not to specific persons, but to functions. We should also follow the Germans. As far as the idea of a separate law is concerned, we gave up this idea because it does not meet the government's expectations for this regulation. It does not comply because such a law would have to be an act that constructs general and abstract regulations on the transformation of institutions such as scientific institutes into cultural institutions. Such an act seems unnecessary in the Polish legal system, because this issue concerns only these two institutions, so we would create a law that would only work in these two cases, and then it would be a dead legal act. It didn't seem right. The second form that such a law could adopt would be that new cultural institutions would be set up by law. That wouldn't provide the continuity I mentioned, that's once, but that's not the most important thing. What's more important is that we think that there's no reason for these institutions to be institutions of such a high rank, which are invoked by a separate law. Moreover, this could make it more difficult to manage these facilities in time. Our intention is to bring about a situation where these institutions can operate on the basis of the regulations of the Act on the organisation and pursuit of cultural activities, such as other such institutions. Therefore, in this law, we propose that this regulation be included in the transitional provision. A separate act, if it had been set up, would have created some practical problems, i.e. Any attempt to change the name or scope of the activities of these institutions in order to adapt their work flexibly to current needs would require a revision of the law and would therefore be a difficult and unpractical process. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u170	We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. We proceed with the examination of item 7 on the agenda: Report from the Health Committee on the Commission's draft law amending the Act on Competitions and Dentist's Doctors and the Act on Patients' Rights and the Ombudsperson of Patients (Prints No. 1998 and 3019). I ask Mr Tadeusz Dziuba to present a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u171	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! The issue of access to medical information related to the patient is governed by the provisions of Article. 40 Act on Doctor's Competition and Art. 14 patient rights laws, the names of the laws are brief. On the other hand, the question of access to patient medical records is governed by the provisions of the article. 26 of these laws, the patient's rights law. The practice of health care institutions and the needs of patients and their loved ones leads to the necessary addition of these provisions, which is the subject of the revision discussed here. First of all, I will discuss an amendment on access to medical information about the patient. In accordance with European standards, the current Polish regulations require those engaged in a medical profession to keep confidential information related to the patient and obtained in connection with the pursuit of the profession. At the same time, they provide for exceptions to this principle. Those persons are bound by a secret also after the patient's death, unless the person close to you consents to the disclosure of the secret. However, if another loved one objects to this consent, there can be no exemption from secrecy after the patient's death. The current Polish legislation does not provide for a solution to such a patch situation and therefore the proposed amendment. The proposed amendment provides for the following additions to the existing provisions. Firstly, it is clear that the patient may object to accessing medical information about him in his lifetime. This objection should then be included in the medical records. The Patient Rights Act expressly stipulates that the patient is entitled to receive information on the consequences of this objection. It is clear that this right will give rise to responsibilities on the side of the management of health care units. Secondly, it is established that, in the event of a dispute between individuals having access to medical information about a patient, consent to access at the request of the person concerned or the doctor concerned shall be given by the court in non-trial proceedings. Your doctor may also go to court if you are not sure if the person concerned is really close to you. The Tribunal may also determine the scope of disclosure of information covered by statutory secrecy. Thirdly, if a patient has objected to the disclosure of medical confidentiality during life, it is determined that the court agrees to break that objection while determining the extent of the disclosure of confidentiality, but only in two cases, namely to protect the life, health of a loved one or a loved one and to seek compensation or compensation for the death of the patient. Fourthly, a framework for the action of the court in these cases shall be established, indicating that the court examines the interests of the parties involved in the proceedings, the actual ties of the loved ones with the deceased patient, the content of his will expressed in life and the circumstances of his opposition in life. In addition, the amendment provides for changes to the symmetry between the provisions on medical confidentiality of patients contained in the two above-mentioned different laws. For so far the rules in this matter, in these two acts, are not fully symmetrical. Therefore, the Patient Rights Act is supplemented, among others. the provision that in situations where medical secrecy can already be disclosed, this can only be done to an unchangeable extent, and in some cases the extent to which confidentiality may be disclosed may be determined by the patient or his or her statutory representative. As I said at the outset, the amendment of the provisions on access to patient medical records requires only amendments to one law, namely the Patient Rights Act. This is the second issue covered by the amendment. In this respect, new provisions are introduced which correspond to the substance of the revision of the provisions on access to medical information. It is established that, after the patient's death, not only the statutory representative of the deceased patient or the person authorised by him or her but also the person close to him will have access to his or her records, unless another person close to him opposes it. In the latter case, the same procedure as previously discussed is established, i.e. the question of access to the records of the deceased patient shall be decided by the court in non-trial proceedings, acting at the request of the person concerned or the person pursuing the medical profession. As before, the court may break the patient's objection to access to his records, an objection expressed, of course, during life, but only in two cases, i.e. to protect the health and life of a loved one and to seek compensation or redress. As before, the amendment establishes a framework for the action of the courts in matters relating to documentation which are identical to those laid down in the case of medical information about the patient. In addition, the concept of a loved one is clarified in the amendment. According to the new definition, it is the spouse, the relative to the second degree, but in a straight line, the statutory representative, the person in common life and the person indicated by the patient. During the work on the draft amendment, there was only one issue at issue, namely the question of breaking the patient's objection, expressed in life, of course, to accessing medical information about him and his medical records. The majority of the members of the Emergency Sub-Committee, and then the majority of the members of the Sejm Committee on Health, advocated that the court, the external arbiter, should have the authority to make such a possible decision within a well-defined decision framework and within a strictly defined framework of procedure. The adoption of such or another solution is not, in fact, due to calculation, but is, in particular, a result of life and work experience. These experiences are diverse and therefore different solutions are different. It is worth looking at how this problem has been resolved in other European countries. It turns out that different solutions have been adopted in different countries, including those similar to those proposed in our amendment. Here are some examples. In Sweden, this problem is addressed on the basis of the application of the recognised good practice, not of strict statutory regulation. The competent health care professional shall recognise the situation and make available the documentation of the person concerned or shall not make it available. If his decision is negative, the application shall, in a second instance, consider the head office of the facility. If that decision is rejected, the appeal authority shall be the administrative court. In Sweden, the disclosure of documents is taken into account, among others. the interests of the deceased and respect for his dignity. Under the laws in force in Denmark, those closest to them may have access to the medical records of the deceased, despite its prior prohibition, if they show that there is an important legal interest in protecting the right of secrecy. This interest may be a question of evidence of medical error or the exclusion of hereditary disease. The decision on these matters shall be taken by the staff member of the health care unit concerned. In the Czech Republic, the scope is governed by the provisions of the law in force. If in this country a patient has prohibited access to his medical records after death during his or her lifetime, his or her loved ones shall have access to it only if it is necessary to protect their health or the health of others. This is the decision taken by the medical staff, and in practice by the treating physician. In the UK, the general rule is to keep the medical records of a deceased patient confidential if he forewarned it while he was alive. However, if access to it is necessary for reasons of overriding public interest, the general principle does not apply. This is the special law on access to medical records. As in the previous cases discussed, the staff of the institution and the medical establishment concerned shall decide on such matters. On the other hand, according to the laws in force in Belgium, the confidentiality of the patient's records is strictly maintained if he has expressly warned himself in his lifetime. As you can see, there is a wide range of solutions in European countries, but there is a large majority of solutions that allow the patient's lack of consent to access medical records and medical information, where the public interest, the interest of a third party or other persons, in particular with regard to the protection of their health, or the supervision of the correctness of the operation of medical personnel, to break the problem. The amendment proposed by us is therefore in line with the solutions applied in many European countries. According to these proposals, the patient has the right to object to third parties' access to medical records and information about him after his death, but at the same time it is possible, only to obtain such access in strictly limited cases. It seems that the most important difference between the solutions proposed in our legal system, proposed in this amendment and applied in other European countries, is that, in the case of disputes, we propose that the court should decide, while in other countries, most of this task is assigned to medical staff. On behalf of the Seym Health Committee, please accept the amendment contained in print 3019. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u173	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Club Law and Justice a position regarding the Commission's draft law amending the Act on Competitions of Doctors and Dentist and the Act on Patients' Rights and the Ombudsperson of Patient Rights, print No 1998. The draft concerns amendments to the rules on the obligation to keep confidential information concerning the patient after his death by medical professionals. An initiative to supplement the provisions on access to medical information about the patient and his medical records was taken by the Sejm Committee on Petitions, submitting the draft in print No 1998. This resulted from requests from family doctors, but also in the interests of people close to them. The reason for this initiative is that the current rules are vague, and implementation of them causes huge problems, and many times in practice, the loved ones do not have the opportunity to obtain the necessary information. The draft of the Committee on Petitions assumed that, after the patient's death, there was a medical confidentiality in respect of the information about him and his medical records, and that the consent to its disclosure could express, and determine the extent of its disclosure, only the court in non-trial proceedings. Breaking medical confidentiality would require a judicial settlement in any case. At the same time, provision was made for the abolition of the existing provision allowing the consent to the disclosure of confidentiality to loved ones, if they are in line with this. The extraordinary subcommittee has made numerous changes in the initial project, cooperating with the Ministry of Health and the Ministry of Justice and drawing on the comments sent to the project and its subsequent modifications. The final version drawn up in print No 3019 is presented by the Health Committee to the Sejm for adoption. In contrast to the initial project in the Commission project, it was assumed that in non-trial proceedings the courts would settle only disputes, i.e. those in which loved ones do not agree with access to medical information about a deceased patient or to his medical records. In non-disputed matters, the consent of loved ones is sufficient, as it is today. As the rapporteur referred to, the draft also provides for the possibility of going to a doctor's court and, more generally, to a medical practitioner. What is very important, the draft committee significantly limits the grounds for the court's action in cases where a patient has objected to the disclosure of secrecy during his lifetime, and it also sets out the framework for the court's action in such situations. I do not refer the draft in detail here, because the rapporteur has done so. The draft submitted to the Sejm is therefore complementary to the current rules, and at the same time it is not casuistic and thus allows good practice to be established in these matters. The only issue at issue during the drafting of the amendment was access to medical confidentiality after the patient's death when he opposed disclosure in his lifetime. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice is in favour of allowing the patient's medical records and medical records to be accessed in such situations. This access will be dealt with by a court, i.e. an arbitrator independent of the parties concerned. We are also aware that to some extent the resolution of this dilemma is arbitrary, i.e. it cannot be clearly demonstrated which solution is more favourable, regardless of whether the patient's lack of consent in life has been observed after death or whether it is possible to break this objection in specific situations. Hence, the decisions made in other countries are important in this regard. Legislation in European countries is uneven in this respect, but it is overwhelming, as the rapporteur pointed out, to allow the patient's failure to agree after his death in special situations. In the case of different and generally quite acute conditions, access to information or medical records of a deceased patient may be permitted despite the opposition he expressed during his life. in the Czech Republic, Denmark, Finland, Spain, Latvia, Germany, Slovakia, Hungary and the United Kingdom. There are also countries where the patient's lack of consent to access to medical confidentiality after his death is strictly respected, such as Ireland, Portugal and Romania. Minister! Dear Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the above position, the Law and Justice Club supports the draft amendment of the law presented in Seym Print No 3019 [[♪ The bell ♪]] and will vote in favour of it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

No charity can provide that. Only the Management Board can use concrete solutions and discipline its staff to implement innovative solutions. It is not without significance that the objectives of the foundation are very vague in the project and it will be very difficult to assess their implementation. Similarly, the way in which staff are employed and the requirements imposed on them do not guarantee the achievement of the objectives pursued. Therefore, as a Civic Platform club, we apply to reject the project in its entirety. Marshal, you're welcome. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u213	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Sorry I'm late, but there's a lot of speed on the agenda. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present an amendment to the Act of 9 June 2011 on support for family and foster care, Parliamentary printing No 3023. What are the main objectives of the changes to the foster care law? The draft law amending the law on family support and foster care and some other laws focuses in particular on family support solutions through the development of services for the care of children, the process of transformation in the area of institutional foster care, the development of family forms of foster care, the issue of the emancipation of adult foster care pupils and the issue of adoption. Since the entry into force of the Act on support of family and foster care system, i.e. since 1 January 2012, the process of transformation in the area of foster care has been gradually implemented in Poland aimed at building the system primarily on family forms of foster care. Care and education institutions will continue to function, but their role is gradually reduced. In 2017, in the family foster care, i.e. foster families and foster homes, there was a total of over 55 thousand. children placed in more than 37 thousand the foster care of family members. In comparison, over 17,000 people stayed in the institutional foster care during the same period. children. The need for revision is therefore primarily due to the need to accelerate the process of change in the area of foster care. Changes in the area of family support focus on creating a wide range of services for a family that raises children, especially a family that suffers from care and education problems, so as to minimise the need to separate the child from the family. Once a child is placed in foster care, it is difficult to accept that children under the age of 7 are still placed in institutional care. We need much more dedicated foster parents to raise children, and therefore we need to propose mechanisms to facilitate them. Another important objective of the Act is to strengthen the tools for the self-emancipation of adult foster care pupils, so that their start in adult life is properly supported and financially supported. Finally, the issue of strengthening procedures aimed at obtaining candidates for adoptive parents, especially ready for adoption in Poland of older children or more siblings. National adoption or a stable foster environment is the solution for children permanently deprived of their parents' care. The latter is intended to further reduce the so-called "deficiencies." international adoptions. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I will also present some detailed solutions that are included in the draft law. The first objective is to strengthen family support tools so that the number of children placed in foster care each year is further gradually reduced and the number of children returning home from foster care per year is growing. This will be served, among other things, by extending the family directory for which the family assistant is dedicated - besides families experiencing difficulties in carrying out care and education functions from the assistant's services, any family in need of support in raising children will be able to benefit, secondly, the compulsory assignment of an assistant to the family in case the child is placed in foster care, thirdly ensuring the possibility of creating branches of a daily support facility e.g. in every village. This solution will provide greater access for children to daily support facilities. In the situation of small rural communities it will be possible to operate a branch even for several children. Second objective: to put in place mechanisms to ensure that new candidates are more effectively recruited to act as a foster family or are willing to run a family home. Here we offer the following facilitations. Firstly, to facilitate the establishment of new foster families. It was pointed out that candidates for foster parents will be able to receive the necessary training and qualifications in each district in Poland, and not only in the district of residence. Secondly, improving the situation of those who create family foster care by introducing the principle that the remuneration of the foster family and the family home of the child must not be lower than the minimum wage for work. Thirdly, the nature of the contract to act as a substitute for a professional family has been changed from the contract for an indefinite period of time. Fourthly, the introduction of an obligation to employ a person to assist in the care and upbringing of foster families and foster homes in the care of at least three children, including at least one with disabilities. The provision follows the demands of foster families raising a child with disabilities. Another objective we want to set out in this Act is to implement the appropriate, stable family environment for younger children, especially those under the age of seven, through, first, the introduction of an electronic system for registering the central places of foster care, foster families, independent persons and the provision of data to organisational units supporting the family and foster care. This solution is intended to serve a better organization of foster care and compatible with the child's welfare in search of spare places of foster care throughout Poland by a family court. This will apply from 2020. Secondly, exceptions are being removed to allow children under 10 years of age to be placed in nursing establishments in order to eliminate the placement of younger children in institutions. Thirdly, the participation of municipalities in the co-financing of children's stay in foster care in the first two years of their stay in a foster family is increasing: first year - 30%, second year - 40% , third and subsequent years - 50% , and the principle of rewarding the municipality in the event of a permanent return of the child to the biological family is introduced. Another problem that we want to address in this law in a special way is the issue of the independence of adult foster care pupils, so as to ensure that pupils are given an effective start in adulthood. This is to be done by the following changes. Firstly, the amount of aid is increased to self-emancipate in the case of an independent person leaving a family of relatives. Secondly, the catalogue of persons who can act as an independent guardian has been extended by adding an NGO employee and another person designated by that organisation to act as an independent guardian. Thirdly, courses and other forms of training provided by the Voluntary Labour Service have been included in the catalogue of possible forms of learning by an independent applicant for further education. And fourthly, the income criterion of PLN 1200 was abolished to enable self-employment and development aid, in order to encourage self-employed people to take up employment. With this change, every single person will receive support. The fifth element of such changes, the specific objectives I will mention, is the issue of facilitating national adoption and strengthening the powers of parents adopting older children. This objective is to be achieved by the following changes. Firstly, increase the role of the Minister responsible for family affairs in the organisation of adoption centres in order to ensure the proper structure of adoption centres in individual voivodships. Secondly, the extension of the catalogue of persons entitled to leave on maternity leave. At present, this right is available to adoptive parents who provide a child up to the age of 7 and, in the case of a child who has been decided to postpone school duties, no longer than until they have completed their 10th year of age. Parents adopting older children will benefit from the proposed solution of 10 or 5 weeks of maternity leave also when adopting older children. Holidays are supposed to be about adapting a child to a new family. We hope that such facilitation may affect the decision to adopt older children. Thirdly, the participation of the Marshal of voivodship and voivodship in the work of the adoption centre. Fourthly, an extension of the period during which parents consent to adoption without the indication of the accomplice. That's so-called. Blanket adoption. The change concerns an extension of up to 10 weeks of the period during which parents consent to adoption without indication of the accomplice. This period is now 6 weeks and seems too short for such an irreversible decision to be taken. Fifthly, changes in foreign adoption, including the possibility of taking over the tasks of the central data bank and introducing a two-step exercise of the tasks of the central authority. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I presented the main assumptions for the bill, as well as the specific objectives of the changes. I ask the High House to work on a law in the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The evolution of foster care in the Polish legal system since the introduction of solutions in 2012 is a road through the torment of many Polish families and children. The three-year district programmes containing the limit of professional foster families for a given calendar year, which often hinders the development of surrogate parenthood in a given county, so that the contract of professional foster family or family home of children with powiats is not an order agreement, but an indefinite contract of employment. Unfortunately, despite the sincere desire and proper rehabilitation of such a child, it often happens that when he is old enough, he is unable to exist on his own. It is very important that children with disabilities who are fostered in foster homes are able to remain in them even after they have reached adulthood on the same principles as foster families, so that they do not have to separate their families and put the sick in a social assistance home. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u246	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the government proposal for a law amending the law on family support and the system of foster care and some other laws. A properly functioning family is the best environment in which a child can grow and grow. If parents or guardians of minors are not able to provide them with good care, a warm and tender home for various reasons, state interference is necessary and the necessary support is directed to such a family. I am pleased that the government, when designing this support, focuses on putting the welfare of the child above all else. This is what the prepared regulations are designed to do, which I mostly appreciate positively. I am convinced that the proposed amendment, among others. on the functions, tasks and competences of family assistants, improve family care and increase its universality, and that the same role will be fulfilled by the regulation on the obligation to listen to the child in the control procedure and that the possibility of setting up a daily support facility will be ensured in the provisions under consideration, e.g. in every village. I also welcome the provisions relating to the introduction of the possibility of paying support families according to the tasks assigned to them, which I am sure will increase the number of families providing such support. Another good solution is to change the rules for the self-emancipation of adult foster parents and to introduce the right to maternity leave for parents adopting older children. I also consider it advisable to adopt regulations to mitigate the requirements for the education of people working in institutions which, under the new rules, will have to have higher education in any direction and to prepare for pedagogical training. As the projectors point out, this solution will increase staffing capacity by exploiting the potential of former teachers. I also have no doubt that its task will be fulfilled by the register of institutional forms of foster care, which will make it quicker and more efficient to search for vacancies in institutions. We proceed with the examination of item 11 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending certain laws in connection with ensuring the application of Regulation 2016/679 (Print No 3050). State Secretary at the Ministry of Digitalization Mr Karol Okoński will present the reasons for the bill. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u267	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill amending certain laws in connection with ensuring the application of Regulation 2016/679, i.e. so-called. GDPR. The project contains changes in sectoral regulations that adapt the Polish legal order to the GDPR regulation, among others. remove provisions that are contrary to the GDPR or duplicate solutions that are contained in the Regulation itself. The project therefore represents a further stage of legislative action aimed at ensuring the effective application of GDPR in Poland. The first step in legislative action was the adoption of the Personal Data Protection Act. This law entered into force on 25 May this year, and Poland was one of the first countries of the European Union to ensure the effective application of GDPR through the adoption of national regulations. In addition to the adoption of the new Act of 10 May 2018 on the protection of personal data, it was necessary to make numerous amendments to other laws. In view of their number and nature, and in order to ensure a coherent approach, it was decided to make them a single bill. In some cases it was necessary to bring national law into line with GDPR by deleting the rules that were not in conformity with it, or duplicated it. It should also be pointed out that GDPR in the field of sensitive data processing or profiling increases requirements and modifies existing rules. It was therefore necessary to review the provisions in this regard and amend them. The draft law adopts, as far as possible, a uniform scheme for the development of personal data regulations. This is a huge value of this sector law and it was also one of the arguments for proceding it in one draft law, not in individual sectoral laws. Thus, thanks to this approach, the bill provides for amendments to 168 laws. It should be stressed that although at this point these changes are formally in one legal act, in practice we will talk not so much about the GDPR Sectoral Law, as could have been the case with the GDPR Implementation Act, but about the specific provisions in the laws to which these amendments concern, i.e. the Code of Administrative Procedure, the Tax Order or the Law on Public Roads. It should also be made clear that the procedure for the law after the start of the application of the GDPR allowed the Council of Ministers to adapt the content of the proposed changes to the real needs, which were largely revealed only in the practice of the application of GDPR in Poland. One example is the amendments to the Criminal Code to help combat the so-called "criminal code." A line of blackmailers. Although of course the proposed changes concern all attempts to extort money, the direct reason for them was what we observed in the first period of application of the new law, GDPR in Poland. Namely, just after the beginning of the application of the GDPR we were filled with information about suspicious offers, in which in a more or less disguised way they tried to blackmail entrepreneurs. That is why we have proposed to extend the definition of a criminal threat. It will threaten not only criminal proceedings but also administrative proceedings resulting in the imposition of a financial penalty. The project also allows patients to request one free copy of all medical records from medical institutions. It is a pro-Citizen solution which at the same time tries to take into account the interests of medical institutions. Sometimes there can be thousands of pages of documents whose preparation and transmission entails considerable financial costs. The Act stipulates that the patient has the right to request the transfer of his records and that the information contained therein concerning doctors does not infringe their rights and freedoms. According to the draft, the authorities will be able to fulfil the information obligation resulting from the GDPR in the first letter addressed to the party. We can talk about a certain shift in the timing of the information obligation. Art. 13 In principle, the GDPR recognises that this obligation should already be fulfilled at the data acquisition stage, but in practice it raises problems. Let's assume - I'm talking about the most obvious case - that the application is being made to the office. By the time it is read, it is not yet known what it is about, and at the same time reading it would mean processing data. Therefore, we have proposed that the public administration should only carry out the full information obligation in the first letter addressed to the party in connection with the proceeding. In our opinion, apart from the burden of public administration, this is in line with the expectations of the citizens themselves. The proposed amendments also concern provisions relating to central registers. For example, the regulations governing the PESEL register explicitly provide for the responsibilities for the PESEL registry of the Minister responsible for IT, the Minister responsible for Home Affairs and the Minister responsible for Foreign Affairs, as well as the responsibilities and access to the register of municipal authorities, the head of the civil status office, passport bodies, voivodeships and ministers: competent for home affairs and competent for computerization. In practice, these changes will facilitate the efficient and efficient implementation of the rights of data subjects under the GDPR, as the rules will clearly indicate which controller to ask for an appropriate request or request. During the legislative work, the question of whether a catalogue of personal data necessary for processing for a particular purpose should be included in each law was also considered. As a result of the analysis, including the analysis of case law, it was considered that the need to regulate in the act of the catalogue of personal data requires the assessment of ad casum each time. Therefore, when analysing whether the nature of the task is the processing of personal data should be determined. If yes, the scope of the processingable data is determined by the statutory task and in this case the directory is not needed. During our legislative work, we have also come to the conclusion that the laws do not always have to make clear who acts as a data controller. Lawyers or lawyers can be an example here. For the purposes of their activities covered by legally protected secrets, they shall be data controllers determining both the purpose of the processing and how they are protected. At the end of this synthetic summary of the main changes, which include, in addition to ordering additional values for citizens, I would also like to point out that this project is one of the biggest ones that have been examined here in recent years. Its creation was only possible thanks to the fruitful cooperation of all resorts. I would like to draw attention to the fact that a significant part of the legislation contained in this project has been prepared by the work of individual ministries, on issues relevant to their areas.
