ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u271	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, I would like to present the position of the club on the government draft law amending certain laws in connection with ensuring the application of Regulation 2016/679, print No 3050. The purpose of this bill is to adapt the Polish legal order to GDPR, among others. by deleting provisions which are contrary to the GDPR or which duplicate GDPR solutions. The aim of the bill is also to adapt the GDPR to the specifics of the Polish legal order. The draft law also introduces provisions which constitute restrictions on GDPR and which have been introduced in accordance with Article. 23 GDPR. They were introduced only to the extent necessary for the proper performance of tasks by the data controllers. The draft law is, in general, a set of changes in the various sectoral regulations that need to be introduced to match the requirements of GDPR and the Personal Data Protection Act itself. The project is extensive and involves amendments to as many as 167 laws, including: sectors such as insurance, banking, culture, health and access to medical records, family and work, sport and tourism, home affairs and administration, environment, national education, investment and development, science and higher education, entrepreneurship and technology and public statistics. The project also includes solutions that respond to a large number of actions aimed at using the personal data protection reform to mislead data controllers. The proposal therefore proposes to amend the Penal Code by judging that the threat of criminal proceedings not only is an unlawful threat, but also another procedure in which an administrative penalty may be imposed. The amendment of the Act is to enter into force 14 days after the date of publication in the Official Journal, except for certain provisions which will become applicable in other time limits. I hope this happens as soon as possible. It is a bit surprising that the fact of proceding such important changes is only now, when the GDPR Act, which has been in force for several months, in connection with other Polish regulations, raises a number of ambiguities and questions. I would like to remind you that the public consultation and opinion of this project took place at the beginning of the year, while the Committee for European Affairs of the Council of Ministers adopted this draft law already on 18 May this year, thus directing it to the meeting of the Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers. Unfortunately, the committee didn't take care of him until November, and only a few days ago, it was accepted by the government. In order to prevent different situations resulting from the lack of prior alignment of our legislation with the EU legislation related to GDPR in the case of a number of specific laws and regulations, we should process the draft law in parallel with the GDPR law. A number of unclear situations could then be avoided and personal data protection rules could be overinterpreted. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u285	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Yesterday there was a meeting of the State Audit Committee, where representatives of the Supreme Audit Chamber presented a generally positive opinion on the exercise of the supervision of State Treasury companies, while one of the specific conclusions was to harmonise the whole system of this supervision. Ladies and gentlemen, the project I am presenting today is in some way in line with the expectations of the Supreme Audit Chamber by introducing a more uniform system of supervision of Treasury companies. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Because the supervisory system, the new system of supervision of companies of the State Treasury, i.e. the concentration under the Prime Minister's authority, has been in force for 2 years. It is now time to assess the solutions put in place. The proposed law on the amendment of the Law on the Principles for the Management of State Property and some other laws aims to correct the shortcomings of the adopted regulations which, in practice, caused the greatest difficulties. This is a reform of this system, which was introduced 2 years ago, a reform necessary to improve the efficiency of the state's functioning in the area of state wealth management. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In the world, there is no single model of company supervision in other countries. In many countries this is based on central coordination. Such practice is in line with the guidelines of international organisations which suggest that the traditional model of supervision is not suitable for dynamically developing state-owned companies. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! With regard to the specific changes envisaged by the project, it concerns three areas in principle. The first area is the powers of the President of the Council of Ministers in the field of supervision. In fact, it is about extending these competences. As far as details are concerned, these competences are clarified, among others. by coordinating the exercise of the powers of the State Treasury in companies and ensuring a uniform way of carrying out tasks for all companies with the State Treasury as a rule. It should be noted that this will not apply to the exercise by a representative of the Government for Foreign Affairs. strategic energy infrastructure rights from shares belonging to the Treasury in relation to the electricity transmission system operator and the gas transmission system operator and PERN. This exclusion is also due to European law. The solutions proposed in the draft change the scope of cases in which the Prime Minister will approve voting instructions at general meetings of shareholders in companies of major importance for the state economy. Until now, these have been mainly property issues and issues relating to the remuneration of the company's authorities. After the adoption of the project, on which the High Chamber is proceeding today, the approval by the President of the Council of Ministers will be subject to cases concerning inter alia. increase and decrease the share capital of the company, merger, merger, division, transformation, dissolution of the company, exclusion of the right to raise new issue shares, sale, lease of the company or its organised part. The draft also provides for the correction of the mechanism for the transfer by the President of the Council of Ministers of powers to exercise rights from shares belonging to the Treasury, since solutions which have been in operation for 2 years have not been interpreted in a uniform manner. The project assumes that this will take place by means of a regulation of the President of the Council of Ministers, which will identify the entity to whom the powers will be delegated and their scope. In the assessment of the applicant, this change will increase the legibility and transparency of the delegation process. In view of the fact that there is no clear indication in the current legal state of the authority authorised to acquire shares on behalf of the Treasury, this competence has been conferred on the President of the Council of Ministers. This solution will also avoid interpretational doubts. Given that the President of the Council of Ministers is responsible for coordinating the supervision of companies with the participation of the State Treasury, the project grants him an additional tool to implement this sentence. It is about the possibility of requesting information on the exercise of the powers of the State Treasury in companies from entities exercising them. Of course, this right will be exercised in compliance with the laws on the protection of statutory information. The second area concerned by the amendment is the issue of the appointment of supervisory authorities in the State Treasury companies. The proposed amendments aim to harmonise the requirements for candidates for supervisory authorities of companies and to focus in a single regulatory act on requirements for persons designated as members of the supervisory authority by a government administration body or a public legal person. As far as further detailed solutions are concerned, from the act on the remuneration of persons managing certain legal entities, so-called. the chimney, the regulation limiting the possibility of being a member of the supervisory body to one company with the majority share of the State Treasury and the local government unit has been transferred. This requirement will also apply to companies with a majority share of the public legal person or municipal legal person and to a shareholder's agent in a limited liability company. These restrictions will apply subject to the provisions of the Anti-corruption Act. In accordance with the proposed amendments, candidates for members of the supervisory authorities of companies as indicated by a government administration body or by a State-owned legal person will be covered by the same requirements as those currently required by persons designated as members of the supervisory authority by the State Treasury or by a State-owned legal person. The creation of a new financial institution, i.e., is another area of substance that is regulated by the amendment presented today. Capital Investment Fund. During the period of application of the Law on the Principles for the Management of State Property, the need to create an isolated, flexible source of financing for capital investment purposes of the Treasury in the form of investments in shares and shares of companies was noted. Therefore, the draft law, on which the High House is currently proceeding, provides for the creation of a new state special purpose fund, i.e. Capital Investment Fund. The funds from this fund will be allocated, among others. for the acquisition and holding of shares by the State Treasury and for the cost of the acquisition of shares. According to the project submitted, the funds of the fund will come from the profits of single-member companies of the State Treasury and from 30% of dividends from shares belonging to the State Treasury. By means of the fund, shares or shares will be acquired in particular in order to implement the economic policy of the State, to increase the shares of the State Treasury in the company and to protect the fundamental interests of the security of the Republic of Poland. In addition to these detailed arrangements which I have presented, the amendment also introduces amendments to 14 laws. We change 30 articles in the State Property Management Act, add 11 new ones. The draft also amends five and introduces three new provisions - these are provisions constituting statutory delegations for the adoption of implementing acts, which will result in the need for eight new implementing regulations in force. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Please accept my explanations. I would also like to ask you to accept the proposed bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u287	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! The amendments introduced will allow for the removal of interpretation doubts which have arisen since the entry into force of the Law on the Principles for the Management of State Property, the clarification of certain of its provisions and the addition of new solutions, which will allow the powers and tasks of government authorities to be organised after the reform of the governance of State property on 1 January 2017 has been introduced and will make the application of the Act more flexible. The need to create a Capital Investment Fund is primarily due to the need to create an isolated, flexible source of financing for capital investment purposes of the Treasury represented by the President of the Council of Ministers in the form of investments in shares and shares of companies. In this context, it is important to indicate a change in the allocation of contributions from the profits of single-member companies of the State Treasury, as the project provides that they will be revenue from the Capital Investment Fund. Although there is no single model of supervision of companies in the world, the analysis of functioning solutions allows to select a set of best practices, whose proper implementation in Poland will significantly improve the model of supervision of companies with the participation of the State Treasury. In many countries, the model of supervision of state-owned companies is based on central coordination. This practice is also in line with the guidelines of international organisations which suggest that the traditional model of supervision is not suitable for dynamically developing state-owned companies. Well, Professor, at this stage, at the pilot stage - and it worked for us at the e-reception - we need to operate in a certain closed ecosystem, because we want to do exactly what the professor asked: we want to study how the system works. That's what piloting is for, to see how it works under real conditions. For this to work effectively, doctors must be convinced to do so, patients must be convinced, and healthcare providers must be convinced to do so. We want to convince the doctors. Here we have quite a small group, so we are able to reach each of them effectively. As an internship professor knows that doctors care very much about their independence. All our experience indicates that if we don't convince them, if we don't face the environment and show that it's worth taking part in this, the doctors will just vote with their legs. As far as patients are concerned, when will the patient want to take a referral? Well, when it does not limit his access to benefits in any way. The question that the professor asked today at the committee meeting and during the previous debate in the plenary room concerned whether this principle of freedom of choice for the provider would be infringed. It will not be affected, but if we tell patients - and in the case of quite narrow specialization, which is sports medicine, it will be possible - that this access is limited to them, because the number of potential healthcare providers in Warsaw is limited, we will be able to convince them to take part in this piloting and without it we will simply have such dry trials in the so-called. formula friend and family. That's not good enough. You can't make the system available to the whole community after it was tested only by our specialists, because life hates vacuum and just seems like software, you always get very insightful tests before. Sometimes it takes up to half the time of the entire manufacturing process. However, I can also say the second thing: I do not know the project, in which despite the great work done during development, despite careful tests, everything worked from the first shift of the shaft and the activation of the system. That's why it has to be done gradually. And it's not just our ideas, but it's environmental expectations, because it's doctors and patients who will use it. And the third point, that is, the conviction of the providers. The more actors we need to cooperate with to ensure this availability, the more difficult it is to convince the healthcare providers, because it is always: Why my hospital, why should my clinic take part in piloting? We want to treat people. But someone has to take part in piloting so that we are able to successfully develop the digital services that Poles are waiting for in every field of life. However, in order for e-sighting to work well, it is necessary to have a very good dictionary in which all possible types of benefits are defined, because without this dictionary in fact the only thing we do is simply take a piece of paper and put it into a computer, not being able to analyse what is happening later, without being able to introduce any automation. Automation is only possible if the data is of high quality and the data is of high quality when there is a good dictionary. In the case of rehabilitation, which - you are absolutely right - should, of course, be covered by e-direction... But today we know that the preparation of this dictionary is complicated because of the number of possible entries in this dictionary, which is why we are putting it off for the next stage. We are counting on this dictionary to be completed next year. Then, by amending the regulation, we will add rehabilitation to the scope covered by e-direction. So that we do not forget about rehabilitation, but we need a little more time to prepare it properly, because please believe me that there will be no consent, approval for these systems, if doctors try to find the type of rehabilitation they want to direct patients to without success. The pilot obviously assumes that there may be errors, but there can be no errors As for assumptions, there can be no fundamental errors, because it will compromise the whole system. ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u327	High Seym! Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I will present my position on the draft law amending certain laws in connection with e-direction and waiting lists for the provision of health care. The aim of this revision is to implement the ongoing pilot e-direction programme and to reduce the waiting time for public-funded healthcare services. The project focuses on the legal extension of the rules for issuing and using the public-funded health care system by electronic means. An important element of the amendment is to change the rules for creating and monitoring queues waiting for patients for specialist and hospital benefits which cannot be provided immediately. Most systemic changes will only apply from 2020 or even 2021. It should be noted that at present, in the event of cancellation of the visit, the provisions provide for the need to propose an exemption for another patient from the list, which often, due to the proximity of the time limit, is not interested in taking advantage of this possibility and, as a result, the released time limit is often not used. The project shall include a solution which will enable any patient for whom the initial period is between 7 and 30 days from the date of his release to propose a visit within that time limit. I share the opinion of the Sejm Analysis Bureau that the bill is not comprehensive and contains vague rules, but deserves support in general. In the 21st century, an integral part of life is the process of computerization in every aspect of human life.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u338	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! My statement concerns the granting of the title to Jelenia Góra City Friendly to Fair Trade. Jelenia Góra was the first in Lower Silesia and the second in Poland, after Poznań, won the title Friendly City for Fair Trade. The certificate was given during the holiday of the city - September Jeleniogórskiego. Thus Jelenia Góra joined the group of over 2 thousand. cities in 34 countries of the world with the title of Fair Trade Towns. This honorable title is the culmination of the six-year activities carried out by the coordinating group of campaigns to promote the idea of fair trade, sustainable development and responsible consumption among the people of Jelenia Góra. The campaign involves and continues to involve the Jelenio Góra schools, kindergartens, cultural units, non-governmental organisations, entrepreneurs and local government, as well as residents. Thanks to the campaign, Jelenio Góra consumers consciously choose ethical products in shops, whose production does not involve violations of human rights, labour rights or environmental degradation. Fair trade products are marked by the international Fairtrade certification system. The most frequently available Fairtrade certified products are coffee, tea, chocolate, cocoa, cane sugar, bananas, but also cotton clothing and offer of these products are constantly increasing. Fair Trade is a bottom-up international social movement aimed at improving the living conditions of small producers from the countries of the global South. Thanks to it, poor people in Asia, Africa, Latin America can work decently, receive an honest price for their products, but they can also establish fair trade relations with a richer part of the world. The Fair Trade Movement is created by NGOs, volunteers, activists and companies and, of great importance, with the participation of consumers. The implementation of fair trade rules has a real impact on tackling poverty, slave labour, including the use of children for hard work, lack of access to markets and dependence on aid received from highly developed countries. The Fair Trade Movement has developed methods to control compliance with Fairtrade standards at all stages of the production and distribution of products through which consumers have a guarantee of origin and quality of goods marked with this mark. Fair trade is not just trade, but a much broader idea of justice in world trade. It points to the need to change traditional trade rules and practices in such a way that people are placed first. In the 21st century fair trade should become the most recognizable form of development aid for the countries of the poor south. I am proud that my city, Jelenia Góra, has won this noble title as a City Friendly to Fair Trade. I urge and encourage ladies and gentlemen and local authorities in cities to engage in the great idea of international solidarity with the people of poor countries in the fair trade movement. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-12-05-sejm-73-1.u340	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On November 25th, 37 years passed since the day when the Podhorążs of the Higher Officer's Fire School in Warsaw launched a strike against the statutory militarization of this formation so that it could be used against society. The strike ended on 2 December 1981 with a brutal attack on the school building from the air in front of the population gathered around the school, the removal of the sick and fictitious liquidation of the school, later established of military status with violations of the laws of that time. The condition for admission to this new, still functioning facility was the signing of a loyalty with the content of: The awareness of the paramilitary nature of the fire service and the related obligations, I declare to take up a course voluntarily at the Fire University. At the same time, I undertake to comply strictly with the obligations arising from the nature of the fire service and the legislation governing university education. Warsaw, the day... 1981, signature. Those who didn't break, didn't sign the Loyalty, weren't allowed to continue their studies, were demoted. They were often affected by chicanes in attempts to study at other universities, as well as in later professional work. To this day the decision on degradation and its consequences has not been revoked, and by refusing to act, officials refer to the rules of martial law. As a result, the period of service before the strike is not a retirement pension. It is one of the social groups, which in no way has been compensated for the harm suffered during communist rule. It's a small group, about 70 sick people. I hope that those who did not strike at that time or decided to sign a loyalty, and who are now in high positions in the fire department, will not block the decision to restore this group of sick people to elementary justice. I hope that the High House will make the necessary changes to the law so that this can be done. Thank you.
